+DEFAULT_WAIT_S = 25.0
+WAIT_ENV = "DOPEMUX_LAUNCH_WAIT"
+POLL_INTERVAL_S = 0.5
+EXIT_REFUSED = 3
+
+TO_SERVICE = "task-orchestrator"
+TO_PORT = 7890
+TO_BACKEND = BackendSpec(f"http://127.0.0.1:{TO_PORT}/mcp", "http", TO_SERVICE)
+TO_WRONG_PROJECT = "TASK_ORCHESTRATOR_START_BLOCKED_WRONG_PROJECT"
+TO_UNKNOWN_OWNER = "TASK_ORCHESTRATOR_START_BLOCKED_UNKNOWN_OWNER"
+OWNER_CHANGED = "LAUNCH_BACKEND_OWNER_CHANGED"
+#: ``docker ps`` budget of one reconnect-guard call (the proxy bounds the call itself too).
+GUARD_DOCKER_TIMEOUT_S = 10.0
+
+Probe = Callable[[BackendSpec], bool]
+
+
+class LaunchError(Exception):
+    """A refusal with a finding code and a one-line remedy."""
+
+    def __init__(self, code: str, remedy: str) -> None:
+        super().__init__(f"{code}: {remedy}")
+        self.code = code
+        self.remedy = remedy
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class LaunchTarget:
+    root: Path
+    identity: ProjectIdentityView
+    service: str
+    spec: Mapping[str, Any]
+    runtime_dir: Path
+    compose_project: str
+    # Carried for the lifecycle call and registry reads; not part of identity.
+    catalog: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict, repr=False, compare=False)
+    registry_path: Optional[Path] = None
+
+
+# --- root -------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+
+def _git_toplevel(base: Path) -> Optional[Path]:
+    if not base.is_dir():
+        return None
+    try:
+        proc = subprocess.run(
+            ["git", "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
+            cwd=base,
+            capture_output=True,
+            text=True,
+            timeout=5,
+            check=False,
+        )
+    except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError):
+        return None
+    top = proc.stdout.strip()
+    if proc.returncode != 0 or not top:
+        return None
+    return Path(top).resolve()
+
+
+def _is_launcher_entry(name: str, entry: Any) -> bool:
+    return (
+        isinstance(entry, dict)
+        and entry.get("type") == "stdio"
+        and entry.get("args") == ["mcp", "launch", name]
+    )
+
+
+def _require_managed_mcp_json(root: Path, service: Optional[str]) -> None:
+    path = root / PROJECT_MCP_FILENAME
+    remedy = f"{path} is not dopemux-rendered; run dopemux mcp repair-config --apply"
+    try:
+        data = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
+    except (OSError, ValueError):
+        raise LaunchError("LAUNCH_MCP_JSON_NOT_MANAGED", remedy) from None
+    servers = data.get("mcpServers") if isinstance(data, dict) else None
+    if not isinstance(servers, dict):
+        raise LaunchError("LAUNCH_MCP_JSON_NOT_MANAGED", remedy)
+    if service is not None:
+        managed = _is_launcher_entry(service, servers.get(service))
+    else:
+        managed = any(
+            _is_launcher_entry(name, entry) for name, entry in servers.items()
+        )
+    if not managed:
+        raise LaunchError("LAUNCH_MCP_JSON_NOT_MANAGED", remedy)
+
+
+def resolve_launch_root(
+    explicit: Optional[Path], *, cwd: Path, service: Optional[str] = None
+) -> Path:
+    """Git top-level of ``explicit or cwd`` whose ``.mcp.json`` is dopemux-rendered.
+
+    With ``service``, that service's entry must be the stdio launcher entry
+    Task 3a renders; without it, at least one entry must be.
+    """
+    base = Path(explicit if explicit is not None else cwd).expanduser()
+    root = _git_toplevel(base)
+    if root is None:
+        raise LaunchError(
+            "LAUNCH_ROOT_NOT_GIT",
+            f"{base} is not inside a git work tree; start the session in a checkout",
+        )
+    _require_managed_mcp_json(root, service)
+    return root
+
+
+# --- target -------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+
+def build_target(
+    root: Path,
+    service: str,
+    *,
+    catalog: Mapping[str, Any],
+    registry_path: Optional[Path] = None,
+) -> LaunchTarget:
+    from .fleet_catalog import catalog_client_shape  # lazy: imports the CLI module
+
+    spec = (catalog.get("servers") or {}).get(service)
+    if not isinstance(spec, Mapping) or catalog_client_shape(dict(spec)) != "launcher":
+        raise LaunchError(
+            "LAUNCH_SERVICE_UNKNOWN",
+            f"{service} is not a launcher service in the MCP catalog; run dopemux mcp doctor",
+        )
+    identity = resolve_identity_view(Path(root))
+    if (
+        not identity.project_id
+        or not identity.project_root
+        or identity.identity_confidence == "UNKNOWN"
+    ):
+        raise LaunchError("PROJECT_IDENTITY_UNKNOWN", "run dopemux mcp doctor")
+    slug = dr.project_slug(Path(identity.project_root).name)
+    return LaunchTarget(
+        root=Path(root),
+        identity=identity,
+        service=service,
+        spec=spec,
+        runtime_dir=runtime_dir_for(identity, registry_path=registry_path),
+        compose_project=dr.compose_project_name(slug, identity.worktree_hash or ""),
+        catalog=catalog,
+        registry_path=registry_path,
+    )
+
+
+# --- backend discovery --------------------------------------------------------
+
+
+def _registry_file(target: LaunchTarget, registry_path: Optional[Path] = None) -> Path:
+    return Path(registry_path or target.registry_path or default_registry_path())
+
+
+def _backend(target: LaunchTarget, ports: Mapping[str, Any]) -> Optional[BackendSpec]:
+    """The sidecar at ``ports``; None when they lack the service's port (never a default)."""
+    clean = {
+        str(k): int(v)
+        for k, v in ports.items()
+        if isinstance(v, int) or str(v).isdigit()
+    }
+    url = sidecar_url(target.spec, clean)
+    if url is None:
+        return None
+    return BackendSpec(url, target.spec["transport"], target.service)
+
+
+def find_backend(
+    target: LaunchTarget, *, registry_path: Optional[Path] = None
+) -> Optional[BackendSpec]:
+    """The newest ``running`` registry record for this worktree and service.
+
+    A record without the service's port is not a backend (the cold path starts
+    through the lifecycle, which records the ports again).
+    """
+    registry = RuntimeRegistry.load(_registry_file(target, registry_path))
+    if registry.parse_status != "OK":
+        return None
+    running = [
+        rec
+        for rec in registry.find(
+            project_id=target.identity.project_id,
+            worktree_root=target.identity.worktree_root,
+            service=target.service,
+        )
+        if rec.get("status") == "running"
+    ]
+    if not running:
+        return None
+    newest = max(running, key=lambda rec: str(rec.get("updated_at") or ""))
+    return _backend(target, newest.get("ports") or {})
+
+
+def _lifecycle_env(
+    env: Optional[Mapping[str, str]], catalog: Mapping[str, Any]
+) -> Dict[str, str]:
+    """Process env for the lifecycle minus ambient identity and catalog port vars.
+
+    Ports come from the lease registry (and the checkout's own envrc, which
+    the lifecycle reads itself), never from another checkout's exports. The
+    identity/port/tenant set is ``docker_runtime.AMBIENT_SCRUB_VARS`` (D3, D5),
+    defined once and shared with the lifecycle's compose environment.
+    """
+    drop = set(dr.AMBIENT_SCRUB_VARS)
+    for spec in (catalog.get("servers") or {}).values():
+        if not isinstance(spec, Mapping):
+            continue
+        if spec.get("port_var"):
+            drop.add(str(spec["port_var"]))
+        for extra in spec.get("extra_port_vars") or []:
+            if isinstance(extra, Mapping) and extra.get("var"):
+                drop.add(str(extra["var"]))
+    source = os.environ if env is None else env
+    return {k: v for k, v in source.items() if k not in drop}
+
+
+#: Env keys whose values are secrets (same markers as the generated mcp.env filter).
+_SECRET_KEY_MARKERS = ("PASSWORD", "SECRET", "TOKEN", "API_KEY", "CREDENTIAL")
+
+
+def _known_secrets(target: LaunchTarget, env: Optional[Mapping[str, str]]) -> List[str]:
+    """Secret values a start failure could echo: the tenant password and secret env."""
+    found: List[str] = []
+    try:
+        creds = ct.load_secrets(ct.secrets_path(str(target.identity.project_id or "")))
+    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - no or unreadable secrets file: nothing to add
+        creds = None
+    if creds is not None and creds.db_password:
+        found.append(creds.db_password)
+    for key, value in (os.environ if env is None else env).items():
+        if (
+            value
+            and len(value) >= 4
+            and any(m in key.upper() for m in _SECRET_KEY_MARKERS)
+        ):
+            found.append(value)
+    return found
+
+
+def _lifecycle_error(
+    result: Any, service: str, secrets: Sequence[str] = ()
+) -> LaunchError:
+    """The refusal for a failed lifecycle start; every echoed text is redacted first."""
+
+    def scrub(text: str, limit: Optional[int] = None) -> str:
+        return ct.redacted_tail(text, secrets, limit=limit)
+
+    for finding in getattr(result, "blocking_findings", None) or []:
+        if finding.get("code"):
+            message = scrub(finding.get("message") or "start blocked")
+            return LaunchError(
+                str(finding["code"]), f"{message}; run dopemux mcp doctor"
+            )
+    for svc in getattr(result, "services", None) or []:
+        for finding in svc.get("findings") or []:
+            if finding.get("severity") in {"ERROR", "FAIL"} and finding.get("code"):
+                return LaunchError(
+                    str(finding["code"]), scrub(str(finding.get("message") or ""))
+                )
+    tail = ""
+    for svc in getattr(result, "services", None) or []:
+        stderr = ((svc.get("result") or {}).get("stderr") or "").strip()
+        if stderr:
+            tail = f" ({scrub(stderr, limit=200)})"
+    return LaunchError(
+        "LAUNCH_START_FAILED",
+        f"dopemux mcp start --services {service} exited {getattr(result, 'exit_code', '?')}"
+        f"{tail}; run dopemux mcp doctor",
+    )
+
+
+def _backend_from_result(target: LaunchTarget, result: Any) -> Optional[BackendSpec]:
+    for svc in getattr(result, "services", None) or []:
+        if svc.get("service") == target.service and isinstance(
+            svc.get("ports"), Mapping
+        ):
+            backend = _backend(target, svc["ports"])
+            if backend is None:
+                raise LaunchError(
+                    "LAUNCH_BACKEND_PORT_UNKNOWN",
+                    f"start reported no {target.spec.get('port_var') or 'port'} for "
+                    f"{target.service}; run dopemux mcp doctor",
+                )
+            return backend
+    return None
+
+
+def _wait_for(
+    backend: BackendSpec,
+    *,
+    probe: Probe,
+    deadline: float,
+    clock: Callable[[], float],
+    sleep: Callable[[float], None],
+) -> BackendSpec:
+    while clock() < deadline:
+        sleep(POLL_INTERVAL_S)
+        if probe(backend):
+            return backend
+    raise LaunchError("LAUNCH_WAIT_EXCEEDED", "run /mcp reconnect all")
+
+
+# --- image policy (D4) ----------------------------------------------------------
+
+
+def build_pid_path(runtime_dir: Path, service: str) -> Path:
+    """``<runtime_dir>/build-<service>.pid``: one per service, so both sidecars may build at once."""
+    return Path(runtime_dir) / f"build-{service}.pid"
+
+
+def _live_build_pid(pid_file: Path) -> Optional[int]:
+    """The pid of a build still running, or None (absent, dead, or older than the build budget).
+
+    The age limit is ``build_timeout_secs()`` (``DOPEMUX_MCP_BUILD_TIMEOUT_SECS``,
+    default 1800 s), the budget a build gets, never the shorter ``up`` budget.
+    """
+    try:
+        pid = int(pid_file.read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip())
+        age = time.time() - pid_file.stat().st_mtime
+    except (OSError, ValueError):
+        return None
+    if pid <= 0 or age > dr.build_timeout_secs() or not _pid_alive(pid):
+        return None  # stale: replaced by the next build
+    return pid
+
+
+def _write_build_pid(pid_file: Path, pid: int) -> None:
+    tmp = pid_file.with_name(f".{pid_file.name}.{os.getpid()}.tmp")
+    tmp.write_text(f"{pid}\n", encoding="utf-8")
+    os.replace(tmp, pid_file)
+
+
+def _build_image_if_missing(target: LaunchTarget) -> None:
+    """Refuse with ``LAUNCH_IMAGE_BUILDING`` while the shared image is missing (D4).
+
+    The first launch spawns one detached ``compose build`` of this service and
+    records its pid in ``<runtime_dir>/build-<service>.pid``; a launch of the same
+    service while that pid is alive spawns nothing. The other service's build is
+    independent: conport and dope-memory may build at once.
+    """
+    if target.spec.get("management_model") != "compose-service":
+        return
+    product = dr.product_root()
+    env = dr.env_with_compose_interpolation(product=product, scrub_ambient=True)
+    image = dr.compose_image_name(target.service, product=product)
+    if dr.docker_image_exists(image, env=env) is not False:
+        return
+    remedy = "building image; retry with /mcp reconnect all in a few minutes"
+    log_path = target.runtime_dir / "build.log"
+    pid_file = build_pid_path(target.runtime_dir, target.service)
+    running = _live_build_pid(pid_file)
+    if running is not None:
+        print(
+            f"dopemux mcp launch: {image} is still building (pid {running}); log {log_path}",
+            file=sys.stderr,
+        )
+        raise LaunchError("LAUNCH_IMAGE_BUILDING", remedy)
+    override, env_file = dr.compose_build_inputs(target.runtime_dir, product=product)
+    command = dr.compose_build_command(
+        product=product,
+        override_path=override,
+        env_file=env_file,
+        project_name=target.compose_project,
+        services=[target.service],
+    )
+    # Append: conport and dope-memory may build at once (separate pid files).
+    with open(log_path, "ab") as log:
+        proc = subprocess.Popen(
+            command,
+            stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL,
+            stdout=log,
+            stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
+            cwd=str(product),
+            env=env,
+            start_new_session=True,
+        )
+    _write_build_pid(pid_file, proc.pid)
+    print(f"dopemux mcp launch: building {image}; log {log_path}", file=sys.stderr)
+    raise LaunchError("LAUNCH_IMAGE_BUILDING", remedy)
+
+
+# --- ensure ---------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+
+def ensure_backend(
+    target: LaunchTarget,
+    *,
+    wait_s: float,
+    lifecycle: Callable[..., Any] = run_lifecycle,
+    probe: Probe = probe_backend,
+    clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic,
+    sleep: Callable[[float], None] = time.sleep,
+    env: Optional[Mapping[str, str]] = None,
+) -> BackendSpec:
+    """A reachable sidecar for ``target`` (conport / dope-memory)."""
+    deadline = clock() + wait_s
+    warm = find_backend(target)
+    if warm is not None and probe(warm):
+        return warm
+    with instance_lock(target.runtime_dir):
+        warm = find_backend(target)
+        if warm is not None and probe(warm):
+            return warm
+        _build_image_if_missing(target)
+        result = lifecycle(
+            "start",
+            repo=target.root,
+            services=[target.service],
+            catalog=target.catalog,
+            registry_path=target.registry_path,
+            process_env=_lifecycle_env(env, target.catalog),
+            trust_env=False,
+            hold_instance_lock=False,  # held above; instance_lock is not re-entrant
+        )
+        if result.exit_code != 0:
+            raise _lifecycle_error(result, target.service, _known_secrets(target, env))
+        backend = _backend_from_result(target, result) or find_backend(target)
+        if backend is None:
+            raise LaunchError(
+                "LAUNCH_START_FAILED",
+                f"start reported no ports for {target.service}; run dopemux mcp doctor",
+            )
+        if probe(backend):
+            return backend
+    return _wait_for(backend, probe=probe, deadline=deadline, clock=clock, sleep=sleep)
+
+
+def _orchestrator_owner_verdict(
+    owners: List[DockerContainerInfo], target: LaunchTarget
+) -> None:
+    """Return when the 7890 container is this project's; raise otherwise."""
+    if len(owners) != 1:
+        raise LaunchError(TO_UNKNOWN_OWNER, "run dopemux mcp doctor")
+    owner = owners[0]
+    observed = identity_from_docker_labels(
+        owner.labels or {}, port=TO_PORT, container_name=owner.name
+    )
+    verdict = match_target(
+        observed,
+        project_id=target.identity.project_id,
+        project_root=target.identity.project_root,
+    )
+    if verdict == "OK":
+        return
+    if verdict == "WRONG_PROJECT":
+        owner_root = observed.project_root or observed.project_id or owner.name
+        raise LaunchError(
+            TO_WRONG_PROJECT,
+            f"{owner_root}; run dopemux mcp switch-project here to take it over",
+        )
+    raise LaunchError(TO_UNKNOWN_OWNER, "run dopemux mcp doctor")
+
+
+def _orchestrator_port_is_ours(
+    target: LaunchTarget, state: Any, port_is_free: Callable[[int], bool]
+) -> bool:
+    """True when this project's orchestrator publishes :7890, False when the port is free.
+
+    Raises ``LaunchError`` for a foreign or unknown owner.
+    """
+    owners = (
+        [c for c in state.containers if TO_PORT in (c.published_ports or [])]
+        if state.available
+        else []
+    )
+    if owners:
+        _orchestrator_owner_verdict(owners, target)
+        return True
+    if not port_is_free(TO_PORT):
+        raise LaunchError(TO_UNKNOWN_OWNER, "run dopemux mcp doctor")
+    return False
+
+
+def ensure_orchestrator(
+    target: LaunchTarget,
+    *,
+    docker: Callable[[], Any] = inspect_running_containers,
+    lifecycle: Callable[..., Any] = run_lifecycle,
+    probe: Probe = probe_backend,
+    port_is_free: Callable[[int], bool] = _port_is_free,
+    wait_s: float = DEFAULT_WAIT_S,
+    clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic,
+    sleep: Callable[[float], None] = time.sleep,
+    env: Optional[Mapping[str, str]] = None,
+) -> BackendSpec:
+    """The reserved-port orchestrator, proven to be this project's (D2, D7)."""
+    deadline = clock() + wait_s
+    with reserved_port_lock(TO_PORT):
+        if _orchestrator_port_is_ours(target, docker(), port_is_free):
+            return TO_BACKEND
+        result = lifecycle(
+            "start",
+            repo=target.root,
+            services=[TO_SERVICE],
+            catalog=target.catalog,
+            registry_path=target.registry_path,
+            process_env=_lifecycle_env(env, target.catalog),
+            trust_env=False,
+            # Explicit: the reserved :7890 lock is held here and is never nested
+            # with launch.lock (locks.py); an orchestrator-only start takes none.
+            hold_instance_lock=False,
+        )
+        if result.exit_code != 0:
+            raise _lifecycle_error(result, TO_SERVICE, _known_secrets(target, env))
+        if probe(TO_BACKEND):
+            return TO_BACKEND
+    return _wait_for(
+        TO_BACKEND, probe=probe, deadline=deadline, clock=clock, sleep=sleep
+    )
+
+
+# --- reconnect guard (C1) ---------------------------------------------------------
+
+
+def _check_sidecar_owner(
+    target: LaunchTarget,
+    port: Optional[int],
+    state: Any,
+    port_is_free: Callable[[int], bool],
+) -> None:
+    """Pass when ``port`` is free or published by this worktree's own sidecar; else raise."""
+    service = target.service
+    if port is None:
+        raise LaunchError(OWNER_CHANGED, f"the {service} backend URL names no port")
+    owners = (
+        [c for c in state.containers if port in (c.published_ports or [])]
+        if state.available
+        else []
+    )
+    if not owners:
+        if port_is_free(port):
+            return  # backend down: the reconnect attempt fails and retries
+        raise LaunchError(
+            OWNER_CHANGED,
+            f":{port} is held by a process that is not this worktree's {service}; "
+            "run dopemux mcp doctor",
+        )
+    labels = (owners[0].labels or {}) if len(owners) == 1 else {}
+    ident = target.identity
+    if (
+        len(owners) != 1
+        or labels.get(dr.LABEL_MANAGED) != "true"
+        or labels.get("dopemux.project_id") != ident.project_id
+        or labels.get("dopemux.worktree_hash") != ident.worktree_hash
+        or labels.get("dopemux.service", service) != service
+    ):
+        names = ", ".join(c.name for c in owners)
+        raise LaunchError(
+            OWNER_CHANGED,
+            f"{names} now publishes :{port} and is not this worktree's {service}; "
+            f"run /mcp reconnect {service}",
+        )
+
+
+def reconnect_guard(
+    target: LaunchTarget,
+    backend: BackendSpec,
+    *,
+    docker: Optional[Callable[[], Any]] = None,
+    port_is_free: Callable[[int], bool] = _port_is_free,
+) -> Callable[[], None]:
+    """The proxy's ``reconnect_guard`` for ``target``: re-prove who owns the backend port.
+
+    task-orchestrator: the docker inspection and owner verdict of
+    ``ensure_orchestrator`` (``TASK_ORCHESTRATOR_START_BLOCKED_*``). conport and
+    dope-memory: the container publishing the backend port must carry
+    ``dopemux.managed=true`` and this instance's ``dopemux.project_id`` and
+    ``dopemux.worktree_hash`` (``LAUNCH_BACKEND_OWNER_CHANGED``). A free port passes:
+    nothing can answer there, so the connect attempt fails and is retried. The
+    proxy calls it before every connect and again after every ``initialize``.
+    """
+
+    def inspect() -> Any:
+        if docker is not None:
+            return docker()
+        return inspect_running_containers(timeout=GUARD_DOCKER_TIMEOUT_S)
+
+    port = urlparse(backend.url).port
+
+    def guard() -> None:
+        try:
+            if target.service == TO_SERVICE:
+                _orchestrator_port_is_ours(target, inspect(), port_is_free)
+            else:
+                _check_sidecar_owner(target, port, inspect(), port_is_free)
+        except LaunchError as exc:
+            raise ProxyRefused(exc.code, exc.remedy) from None
+
+    return guard
+
+
+# --- launch -----------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+
+def _load_catalog(root: Optional[Path] = None) -> Dict[str, Any]:
+    """The MCP catalog for ``root``: ``$DOPEMUX_MCP_CATALOG``, else its repo-local one, else bundled."""
+    import click
+
+    from dopemux.commands.mcp_commands import _load_catalog as load
+
+    try:
+        catalog: Dict[str, Any] = load(repo=root)
+    except click.ClickException as exc:
+        raise LaunchError("LAUNCH_CATALOG_INVALID", exc.format_message()) from None
+    return catalog
+
+
+# A launcher runs inside Claude's connect budget: one candidate, short docker calls
+# (each also capped at the time left), all within 8 s.
+LAUNCH_REAP_LIMITS: Dict[str, Any] = {
+    "max_candidates": 1,
+    "stop_time_s": 2,
+    "cmd_timeout_s": 3.0,
+    "deadline_s": 8.0,
+}
+
+#: Exit codes of a launcher ended by a signal (128 + signal number).
+_SIGNAL_EXITS = {"SIGTERM": 143, "SIGHUP": 129}
+
+
+@contextlib.contextmanager
+def _signals_exit_through_finally() -> Iterator[None]:
+    """SIGTERM/SIGHUP end the launch with 143/129 through ``finally``; restored on exit.
+
+    Outside an event loop the handler raises ``SystemExit(128 + signum)``. Inside
+    one (a backend probe or the proxy) raising there is unsafe (an exception group,
+    or a loop shutdown that never finishes), so the handler defers: the loop is
+    cancelled at a safe point and ``run_proxy`` returns the code, or
+    ``probe_backend`` raises it once its loop has ended. Only the main thread can
+    install handlers; elsewhere this does nothing.
+    """
+    installed: Dict[int, Any] = {}
+    stdio_proxy.reset_deferred_exit()
+
+    def _exit(signum: int, _frame: Any) -> None:
+        if stdio_proxy.defer_exit_in_event_loop(128 + signum):
+            return
+        raise SystemExit(128 + signum)
+
+    if threading.current_thread() is threading.main_thread():
+        for name in _SIGNAL_EXITS:
+            signum = getattr(signal, name, None)
+            if signum is not None:
+                installed[signum] = signal.signal(signum, _exit)
+    try:
+        yield
+    finally:
+        for signum, previous in installed.items():
+            signal.signal(signum, previous)
+
+
+def _instance_marker_fields(target: LaunchTarget) -> Optional[Dict[str, str]]:
+    """The identity that names ``target.runtime_dir`` (the values ``runtime_dir_for`` uses).
+
+    ``None`` when the identity cannot name it (no project id or worktree root):
+    such a marker would be malformed, so nothing is written.
+    """
+    ident = target.identity
+    project_id = str(getattr(ident, "project_id", None) or "")
+    worktree_root = str(getattr(ident, "worktree_root", None) or "")
+    if not project_id or not worktree_root:
+        return None
+    worktree_hash = getattr(ident, "worktree_hash", None) or instance_id_for_path(
+        worktree_root
+    )
+    return {
+        "project_id": project_id,
+        "worktree_hash": str(worktree_hash),
+        "worktree_root": worktree_root,
+        "project_root": str(getattr(ident, "project_root", None) or worktree_root),
+    }
+
+
+def _write_instance_marker(target: LaunchTarget) -> None:
+    """Best effort: ``<runtime_dir>/instance.json`` names the instance owning the dir.
+
+    Reap finds a runtime dir that never got a registry entry (a launch refused with
+    ``LAUNCH_IMAGE_BUILDING``) through it once the worktree is gone. Written only
+    when missing or different, atomically (tmp file, then ``os.replace``), mode
+    0600; identity fields and a timestamp only, never env or secrets. The caller
+    holds the instance lock. An ``OSError`` prints one stderr line and never
+    refuses the launch.
+    """
+    fields = _instance_marker_fields(target)
+    if fields is None:
+        return
+    marker = target.runtime_dir / INSTANCE_MARKER
+    try:
+        current = json.loads(marker.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
+    except (OSError, ValueError):
+        current = None
+    if isinstance(current, dict) and all(
+        current.get(key) == fields[key] for key in INSTANCE_MARKER_FIELDS
+    ):
+        return
+    tmp = marker.with_name(f".{INSTANCE_MARKER}.{os.getpid()}.tmp")
+    try:
+        with contextlib.suppress(FileNotFoundError):
+            tmp.unlink()
+        fd = os.open(tmp, os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL, 0o600)
+        with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
+            json.dump({**fields, "written_at": _utc_stamp()}, handle, sort_keys=True)
+            handle.write("\n")
+        os.replace(tmp, marker)
+    except OSError as exc:
+        with contextlib.suppress(OSError):
+            tmp.unlink()
+        print(
+            f"dopemux mcp launch: could not write {marker} ({exc}); continuing",
+            file=sys.stderr,
+            flush=True,
+        )
+
+
+def _run_reaper(reaper: Optional[Callable[[], Any]]) -> None:
+    try:
+        if reaper is not None:
+            reaper()
+        else:
+            maybe_reap(**LAUNCH_REAP_LIMITS)
+    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - cleanup must never block a launch
+        print(f"reap skipped: {exc}", file=sys.stderr, flush=True)
+
+
+def _wait_seconds(env: Mapping[str, str]) -> float:
+    try:
+        value = float(env.get(WAIT_ENV) or DEFAULT_WAIT_S)
+    except ValueError:
+        return DEFAULT_WAIT_S
+    return value if value > 0 else DEFAULT_WAIT_S
+
+
+def _process_cwd() -> Path:
+    try:
+        return Path.cwd()
+    except FileNotFoundError:
+        raise LaunchError(
+            "LAUNCH_ROOT_NOT_GIT", "the working directory no longer exists"
+        ) from None
+
+
+def launch(
+    service: str,
+    *,
+    root: Optional[Path] = None,
+    env: Optional[Mapping[str, str]] = None,
+    registry_path: Optional[Path] = None,
+    reaper: Optional[Callable[[], Any]] = None,
+) -> int:
+    """Run the launcher for ``service``; returns the process exit code.
+
+    ``env`` defaults to ``os.environ`` as it is when called. ``reaper`` defaults to
+    ``reap.maybe_reap`` (throttled, kill-switchable, capped at one candidate, 8 s
+    deadline); it runs after this launcher's attach record exists, so its own
+    instance is never reaped, and outside the instance lock. A reap failure never
+    refuses a launch. ``root`` (``--root``) picks the checkout and its catalog.
+    """
+    if env is None:
+        env = os.environ
+    record: Optional[Path] = None
+    with _signals_exit_through_finally(), quiet_sdk_loggers():
+        try:
+            # stdout is the MCP channel: nothing before the proxy may print there.
+            with contextlib.redirect_stdout(sys.stderr):
+                launch_root = resolve_launch_root(
+                    root, cwd=_process_cwd(), service=service
+                )
+                target = build_target(
+                    launch_root,
+                    service,
+                    catalog=_load_catalog(launch_root),
+                    registry_path=registry_path,
+                )
+                # D8: the attach record is written under the instance lock, so a reap
+                # that holds the lock sees either the record or none of this launch.
+                # Released before the reaper (the reap takes the lock itself) and
+                # before ensure_backend (which takes it again; it is not re-entrant).
+                # The instance.json marker is written under the same lock, so a
+                # build-only launch's dir is reapable once its worktree is gone.
+                with instance_lock(target.runtime_dir):
+                    record = attach(target.runtime_dir)
+                    _write_instance_marker(target)
+                _run_reaper(reaper)
+                wait_s = _wait_seconds(env)
+                if service == TO_SERVICE:
+                    backend = ensure_orchestrator(target, wait_s=wait_s, env=env)
+                else:
+                    backend = ensure_backend(target, wait_s=wait_s, env=env)
+                guard = reconnect_guard(target, backend)
+            return run_proxy(backend, reconnect_guard=guard)
+        except LaunchError as exc:
+            print(f"{exc.code}: {exc.remedy}", file=sys.stderr, flush=True)
+            return EXIT_REFUSED
+        except OSError as exc:
+            # attach, build.log, a lock that never stabilised: a refusal, not a traceback
+            print(
+                f"LAUNCH_IO_ERROR: {exc}; retry, and run dopemux mcp doctor if it persists",
+                file=sys.stderr,
+                flush=True,
+            )
+            return EXIT_REFUSED
+        finally:
+            if record is not None:
+                detach(record)

+                    check=False,
+                )
+            except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError):
+                proc = None
+            if proc is not None and proc.returncode == 0 and proc.stdout.strip():
+                path = Path(proc.stdout.strip()).resolve()
     if not path.is_dir():
         raise FileNotFoundError(f"Target path not found: {path}")
     return path

+class PortAllocationBlocked(RuntimeError):
+    """Lease allocation refused; ``findings`` carries the allocator's reasons."""
+
+    def __init__(self, message: str, findings: Optional[List[Dict[str, Any]]] = None) -> None:
+        super().__init__(message)
+        self.findings: List[Dict[str, Any]] = list(findings or [])
+
+
+def _port_vars_for(name: str, catalog: Mapping[str, Any]) -> List[str]:
+    spec = (catalog.get("servers") or {}).get(name) or {}
+    if not isinstance(spec, dict):
+        return []
+    out = [spec.get("port_var")] + [
+        e.get("var") for e in (spec.get("extra_port_vars") or []) if isinstance(e, dict)
+    ]
+    return [str(v) for v in out if v]
+
+
+def _leased_ports(
+    lease_reg: PortLeaseRegistry,
+    identity: ProjectIdentityView,
+    wanted: Sequence[str],
+) -> Dict[str, int]:
+    """Active leases that belong to this project/worktree, keyed by port env var."""
+    found: Dict[str, int] = {}
+    for lease in lease_reg.active_leases():
+        var = lease.get("port_var")
+        if var not in wanted or not lease.get("port"):
+            continue
+        if lease_reg.identity_matches(
+            lease,
+            worktree_root=identity.worktree_root,
+            project_root=identity.project_root,
+            worktree_hash=identity.worktree_hash,
+            project_id=identity.project_id,
+        ):
+            found[str(var)] = int(lease["port"])
+    return found
+
+
+def _service_for_port_var(var: str, catalog: Mapping[str, Any]) -> Optional[str]:
+    for name in (catalog.get("servers") or {}):
+        if var in _port_vars_for(str(name), catalog):
+            return str(name)
+    return None
+
+
+def _leases_held_by_other_service(
+    leased: Mapping[str, int],
+    catalog: Mapping[str, Any],
+    docker: Optional[di.DockerInspectResult],
+    project_id: Optional[str],
+) -> Dict[str, str]:
+    """``{port_var: owner}`` for leased ports published by another service's container.
+
+    Same condition as the ``DOCKER_CONTAINER_PORT_COLLISION`` service-label check
+    in ``_collision_checks``: managed, this ``dopemux.project_id``, and a
+    ``dopemux.service`` that is not the service the port variable belongs to.
+    """
+    if docker is None or not docker.available or not project_id:
+        return {}
+    owners: Dict[int, di.DockerContainerInfo] = {}
+    for c in docker.containers:
+        for published in c.published_ports or []:
+            owners[int(published)] = c
+    held: Dict[str, str] = {}
+    for var, port in leased.items():
+        owner = owners.get(int(port))
+        if owner is None:
+            continue
+        labels = owner.labels or {}
+        service = _service_for_port_var(var, catalog)
+        owner_service = labels.get("dopemux.service") or ""
+        if (
+            labels.get(dr.LABEL_MANAGED) == "true"
+            and labels.get("dopemux.project_id") == project_id
+            and service
+            and owner_service
+            and owner_service != service
+        ):
+            held[var] = f"{owner.name} (service={owner_service})"
+    return held
+
+
+def _ports_published_by_this_instance(
+    docker: Optional[di.DockerInspectResult], identity: ProjectIdentityView
+) -> Set[int]:
+    """Host ports the running, managed containers of this worktree instance publish."""
+    if docker is None or not docker.available:
+        return set()
+    out: Set[int] = set()
+    for c in docker.containers:
+        labels = c.labels or {}
+        if (
+            labels.get(dr.LABEL_MANAGED) == "true"
+            and labels.get("dopemux.project_id") == identity.project_id
+            and labels.get("dopemux.worktree_hash") == identity.worktree_hash
+        ):
+            out.update(int(p) for p in (c.published_ports or []))
+    return out
+
+
+def _resolve_ports(
+    identity: ProjectIdentityView,
+    selected: Sequence[str],
+    catalog: Mapping[str, Any],
+    *,
+    lease_path: Path,
+    source: str = "dopemux mcp start",
+    env: Optional[Mapping[str, str]] = None,
+    allocate: bool = True,
+    is_free_fn: Optional[Callable[[int], bool]] = None,
+    docker: Optional[di.DockerInspectResult] = None,
+) -> Dict[str, int]:
+    """Port map ``{port_env_var: port}`` for ``selected`` services.
+
+    Order without ``allocate``: ports an envrc/process env already names win;
+    otherwise the active leases bound to this project/worktree. With ``allocate``
+    the next paragraph decides which env ports count, and the rest are allocated
+    and persisted as new leases under ``registry_lock``. The lock is held
+    only for the release/allocate-and-save step. Task Orchestrator is a reserved
+    singleton port, never leased, and is left to the caller's default.
+
+    With ``allocate`` (Task B7-2), an env/envrc port of a leased service counts only
+    when this identity holds an active lease for that variable on that very port
+    (``_leased_ports``). Otherwise it is a preferred value for the locked
+    allocation: an existing lease of this identity for the variable wins; with none
+    (``init`` wrote the envrc, then a ``stop`` released the leases) the preferred
+    port is leased when no other lease holds it and the socket is free (a port this
+    worktree's own running sidecar publishes counts as free), else a fresh one, and
+    the returned port replaces the envrc's (the envrc is informational). Without
+    ``allocate`` (status, doctor, stop) env ports are returned as before.
+
+    With ``docker`` and ``allocate``, a leased port that a same-project container
+    of another service publishes (a cross-service collision an older
+    one-service-at-a-time allocation could write) is released and re-allocated
+    instead of failing the start.
+    """
+    ports = _extract_ports(env or {}, selected, catalog)
+    if allocate and identity.worktree_root and identity.project_root:
+        lease_vars = [
+            v
+            for n in selected
+            if n != "task-orchestrator"
+            for v in _port_vars_for(n, catalog)
+        ]
+        held = _leased_ports(PortLeaseRegistry.load(lease_path), identity, lease_vars)
+        for var in lease_vars:
+            if var in ports and held.get(var) != ports[var]:
+                # Not backed by this identity's lease for that port: a preference
+                # only. `existing_envrc=env` below still offers it to the allocator,
+                # which reuses this identity's lease for the variable if it has one,
+                # else leases the preferred port if it is free and unclaimed.
+                del ports[var]
+    lease_services = [
+        n
+        for n in selected
+        if n != "task-orchestrator" and any(v not in ports for v in _port_vars_for(n, catalog))
+    ]
+    if not lease_services or not identity.worktree_root or not identity.project_root:
+        return ports
+
+    wanted = [v for n in lease_services for v in _port_vars_for(n, catalog)]
+    current = _leased_ports(PortLeaseRegistry.load(lease_path), identity, wanted)
+    leased = {var: port for var, port in current.items() if var not in ports}
+    stale = _leases_held_by_other_service(leased, catalog, docker, identity.project_id)
+    if not allocate:
+        stale = {}
+    for var, port in leased.items():
+        if var not in stale:
+            ports.setdefault(var, port)
+    lease_services = [
+        n for n in lease_services if any(v not in ports for v in _port_vars_for(n, catalog))
+    ]
+    if not lease_services or not allocate:
+        return ports
+
+    from .port_allocator import allocate_ports, port_is_free
+
+    # B7-2: a port this worktree's own running sidecar publishes is not "taken": a
+    # preferred envrc port it holds (its lease was lost) is leased as it is.
+    own_ports = _ports_published_by_this_instance(docker, identity)
+    base_is_free = is_free_fn or port_is_free
+
+    def own_or_free(port: int) -> bool:
+        return int(port) in own_ports or base_is_free(port)
+
+    alloc_is_free: Optional[Callable[[int], bool]] = (
+        own_or_free if own_ports else is_free_fn
+    )
+
+    # Exactly the call `mcp_commands._allocate_ports` makes (allocate_ports_map):
+    # no `identity=`, so the allocator derives the same lease identity as `init`.
+    with registry_lock(lease_path):
+        if stale:
+            leases = PortLeaseRegistry.load(lease_path)
+            for lease in leases.active_leases():
+                var = str(lease.get("port_var") or "")
+                if (
+                    var in stale
+                    and int(lease.get("port") or 0) == leased[var]
+                    and leases.identity_matches(
+                        lease,
+                        worktree_root=identity.worktree_root,
+                        project_root=identity.project_root,
+                        worktree_hash=identity.worktree_hash,
+                        project_id=identity.project_id,
+                    )
+                ):
+                    leases.mark_released(lease_id=str(lease.get("lease_id")))
+            leases.save()
+        result = allocate_ports(
+            lease_services,
+            catalog,
+            worktree=identity.worktree_root,
+            project_root=identity.project_root,
+            existing_envrc=env,
+            registry_path=lease_path,
+            persist=True,
+            dry_run=False,
+            source=source,
+            is_free_fn=alloc_is_free,
+        )
+    if result.status == "BLOCKED":
+        msgs = "; ".join(f["message"] for f in result.blocking_findings if f.get("message"))
+        raise PortAllocationBlocked(
+            msgs or "Port allocation blocked", result.blocking_findings
+        )
+    for var, port in result.ports.items():
+        ports.setdefault(str(var), int(port))
+    return ports
+
+
+def runtime_dir_for_paths(
+    *, project_root: str | Path, worktree_hash: str, registry_path: Optional[Path] = None
+) -> Path:
+    """The one rule for a worktree's runtime dir: ``<registry dir>/<slug>-<worktree_hash>``."""
+    slug = dr.project_slug(Path(project_root).name)
+    base = registry_path or default_registry_path()
+    return base.parent / f"{slug}-{worktree_hash}"
+
+
+def runtime_dir_for(
+    identity: ProjectIdentityView, *, registry_path: Optional[Path] = None
+) -> Path:
+    """Per-worktree runtime dir: ``<registry dir>/<slug>-<worktree_hash>``.
+
+    Shared by the start path, the launcher and reap so they agree on one path.
+    """
+    worktree_root = identity.worktree_root
+    project_root = identity.project_root or worktree_root
+    if not worktree_root or not project_root:
+        raise ValueError("identity has no worktree_root/project_root")
+    worktree_hash = identity.worktree_hash or instance_id_for_path(worktree_root)
+    return runtime_dir_for_paths(
+        project_root=project_root, worktree_hash=worktree_hash, registry_path=registry_path
+    )
+

+@dataclass
+class _Facts:
+    """The mutable preflight facts of a lifecycle run and the findings derived from them."""
+
+    registry: RuntimeRegistry
+    docker: di.DockerInspectResult
+    ports: Dict[str, int]
+    blocking: List[Dict[str, Any]]
+    warnings: List[Dict[str, Any]]
+
+
+def _mutable_facts(
+    op: str,
+    *,
+    orchestrator_gate: bool,
+    identity: ProjectIdentityView,
+    target: Path,
+    selected: Sequence[str],
+    catalog: Mapping[str, Any],
+    reg_path: Path,
+    docker_runner: Optional[Callable[..., Any]],
+    doctor_env: Mapping[str, str],

+    project_id: str,
+    worktree_root: str,
+    blocking: Sequence[Dict[str, Any]],
+    warnings: Sequence[Dict[str, Any]],
+) -> _Facts:
+    """Registry, docker snapshot and ports, with their findings appended (deduplicated).
+
+    ``blocking``/``warnings`` are the findings that do not change (identity, envrc,
+    ``.mcp.json``, doctor). One helper for the preflight and for the re-check under
+    the instance lock (B2 fix round 1), so both compute the same thing. The
+    task-orchestrator gate (``orchestrator_gate``) runs only in the preflight: the
+    orchestrator part never runs under the instance lock and re-checks its owner
+    under the reserved lock itself.
+    """
+    blocking = list(blocking)
+    warnings = list(warnings)
+
+    # Load registry
+    if op == "status":
+        registry = RuntimeRegistry.load(reg_path, create_missing=False)
+    else:
+        registry = RuntimeRegistry.load(reg_path, create_missing=False)
+        if registry.parse_status == "ERROR" and op in {"start", "stop", "restart"}:
             blocking.append(
                 {
-                    "code": "TASK_ORCHESTRATOR_START_BLOCKED_UNKNOWN_OWNER",
+                    "code": "REGISTRY_PARSE_ERROR",
                     "severity": "FAIL",
-                    "message": f"TO identity preflight failed: {exc}",
-                    "service": "task-orchestrator",
+                    "message": f"Registry unreadable: {registry.error}",
+                    "service": None,
                 }
             )
 
-    # Lease registry consistency (start only — recommend repair-config on mismatch)
-    if op == "start":
+    docker = di.inspect_running_containers(runner=docker_runner)
+
+    try:
+        ports = _resolve_ports(
+            identity,
+            selected,
+            catalog,
+            lease_path=default_lease_registry_path(),
+            env=doctor_env,
+            allocate=op in {"start", "restart"},
+            is_free_fn=is_free,
+            docker=docker,
+        )
+    except PortAllocationBlocked as exc:
+        blocking.append(
+            {
+                "code": "PORT_ALLOCATION_BLOCKED",
+                "severity": "FAIL",
+                "message": str(exc),
+                "service": None,
+            }
+        )
+        blocking.extend(exc.findings)
+        ports = _extract_ports(doctor_env, selected, catalog)
+
+    # Task Orchestrator fixed-port identity gate (Packet 005). `_start_services`
+    # repeats it under the reserved lock right before the TO start.
+    if (
+        orchestrator_gate
+        and op in {"start", "restart"}
+        and "task-orchestrator" in selected
+    ):
+        to_blocking, to_warnings = _to_start_gate(
+            identity,
+            docker,
+            catalog=catalog,
+            ports=ports,
+            is_free=is_free,
+            prior_blocking=blocking,
+        )
+        blocking.extend(to_blocking)
+        warnings.extend(to_warnings)
+

+    if op == "start":
         try:
             from .port_leases import PortLeaseRegistry

+    )
+    if op in {"start", "restart"}:
+        blocking.extend(collision_findings)
+
+    # Deduplicate blocking by code+service+message
+    seen_b = set()
+    uniq_blocking = []
+    for f in blocking:
+        key = (f.get("code"), f.get("service"), f.get("message"))
+        if key in seen_b:
+            continue
+        seen_b.add(key)
+        uniq_blocking.append(f)
+    blocking = uniq_blocking
+
+    return _Facts(
+        registry=registry,
+        docker=docker,
+        ports=ports,
+        blocking=blocking,
+        warnings=warnings,
+    )
+
+
+def _docker_unavailable_finding(
+    snapshot: di.DockerInspectResult, service: Optional[str] = None
+) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
+    """``[DOCKER_UNAVAILABLE]`` when ``snapshot`` could not be read, else ``[]``.
+
+    Fix round 4: a snapshot re-read under or after the instance lock must fail
+    closed. An unreadable docker lists no container, which would read as "already
+    stopped" or "not running" and release state or start blindly.
+    """
+    if snapshot.available:
+        return []
+    return [
+        {
+            "code": "DOCKER_UNAVAILABLE",
+            "severity": "FAIL",
+            "message": snapshot.error or "Docker unavailable",
+            "service": service,
+        }
+    ]
+
+
+def _docker_failed_part(
+    op: str,
+    services: Sequence[str],
+    findings: List[Dict[str, Any]],
+    *,
+    not_done: str,
+    identity_dict: Dict[str, Any],
+    reg_path: Path,
+) -> LifecycleResult:
+    """FAIL for ``services`` whose docker snapshot could not be read (nothing was run)."""
+    return LifecycleResult(
+        schema_version=SCHEMA_VERSION,
+        operation=op,
+        dry_run=False,
+        status="FAIL",
+        project_identity=identity_dict,
+        services=[
+            {
+                "service": svc,
+                "action": "failed",
+                "reason": f"docker could not be read; not {not_done}",
+                "findings": list(findings),
+                "commands": [],
+            }
+            for svc in services
+        ],
+        blocking_findings=list(findings),
+        registry_path=str(reg_path),
+        exit_code=1,
+        recommended_next_actions=[
+            "Check that Docker answers (dopemux mcp doctor), then retry.",
+        ],
+    )
+
+
+#: Findings that still block a ``stop`` (it needs no envrc, ports or free ports).
+_STOP_HARD_CODES = frozenset(
+    {"REGISTRY_PARSE_ERROR", "PROJECT_IDENTITY_UNKNOWN", "DOCKER_UNAVAILABLE"}
+)
+
+
+def _stop_blocking(blocking: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
+    """A stop's blocking findings: all of them when one is a hard one, else none.
+
+    For stop, only hard safety blocks (it does not need an envrc when the registry
+    has entries).
+    """
+    if any(f.get("code") in _STOP_HARD_CODES for f in blocking):
+        return blocking
+    return []
+
+
+def _blocked_result(
+    op: str,
+    *,
+    dry_run: bool,
+    selected: Sequence[str],
+    blocking: List[Dict[str, Any]],
+    warnings: List[Dict[str, Any]],
+    container_names: Mapping[str, str],
+    labels_by_service: Mapping[str, Dict[str, str]],
+    ports: Mapping[str, int],
+    catalog: Mapping[str, Any],
+    identity_dict: Dict[str, Any],
+    reg_path: Path,
+) -> LifecycleResult:
+    """The FAIL (``BLOCKED`` when dry) result of a run stopped by ``blocking`` findings."""
+    svc_plans = [
+        {
+            "service": s,
+            "action": "blocked",
+            "reason": "preflight blocked",
+            "container_name": container_names.get(s),
+            "ports": {k: v for k, v in ports.items() if s.split("-")[0] in k.lower() or True},
+            "labels": labels_by_service.get(s, {}),
+            "runtime_files": [],
+            "commands": [],
+            "preflight_findings": [f for f in blocking if f.get("service") in (None, s)],
+        }
+        for s in selected
+    ]
+    # simplify ports in plan
+    for plan in svc_plans:
+        plan["ports"] = _ports_for_service(plan["service"], ports, catalog)
+    return LifecycleResult(
+        schema_version=SCHEMA_VERSION,
+        operation=op,
+        dry_run=dry_run,
+        status="BLOCKED" if dry_run else "FAIL",
+        project_identity=identity_dict,
+        services=svc_plans,
+        blocking_findings=blocking,
+        warnings=warnings,
+        registry_path=str(reg_path),
+        exit_code=1,
+        recommended_next_actions=[
+            "Fix blocking preflight findings (doctor --json), then retry.",
+            "Do not start via cwd compose env injection.",
+        ],
+    )
+
+
+def run_lifecycle(
+    operation: str,
+    *,
+    repo: Optional[str | Path] = None,
+    services: Optional[Sequence[str]] = None,
+    catalog: Mapping[str, Any],
+    dry_run: bool = False,
+    registry_path: Optional[Path] = None,
+    docker_runner: Optional[Callable[..., Any]] = None,
+    cmd_runner: Optional[Callable[..., Any]] = None,
+    skip_doctor: bool = False,
+    process_env: Optional[Mapping[str, str]] = None,
+    port_is_free_fn: Optional[Callable[[int], bool]] = None,
+    product_root: Optional[Path] = None,
+    trust_env: bool = False,
+    hold_instance_lock: bool = True,
+) -> LifecycleResult:
+    """Execute start|stop|restart|status for project-scoped MCP sidecars.
+
+    ``hold_instance_lock`` (default): a non-dry ``stop`` runs under the worktree's
+    ``instance_lock``, and so do the worktree-scoped services of a ``start`` or
+    ``restart`` (one acquisition for a restart's stop and start). The
+    task-orchestrator part always runs outside it (B2: the reserved and instance
+    locks are never nested). Callers that
+    already hold the instance lock (the launcher) pass ``False``.
+    """
+    op = operation.lower().strip()
+    if op not in {"start", "stop", "restart", "status"}:
+        raise ValueError(f"unsupported operation: {operation}")
+
+    target = resolve_target_repo(repo)
+    is_free = port_is_free_fn or _port_is_free
+    envrc = load_envrc(target / ENVRC_FILENAME)
+    doctor_env = merge_envrc_into_environ(dict(process_env or {}), envrc, override=True)
+    mcp_json = load_mcp_json(target / PROJECT_MCP_FILENAME)
+    mcp_servers = mcp_json.get("servers") or {}
+    identity = resolve_identity_view(target, envrc_values=doctor_env, trust_env=trust_env)
+
+    selected = select_services(services, mcp_servers, catalog)
+    reg_path = registry_path or default_registry_path()
+
+    # Doctor preflight (read-only; never creates registry)
+    doctor: Optional[DoctorReport] = None
+    if not skip_doctor and op != "status":
+        doctor = run_mcp_doctor(
+            target,
+            catalog=catalog,
+            skip_docker=False,
+            skip_port_probe=True,
+            process_env=doctor_env,
+            trust_env=trust_env,
+            docker_runner=docker_runner,
+        )
+    elif op == "status":
+        doctor = run_mcp_doctor(
+            target,
+            catalog=catalog,
+            skip_docker=False,
+            skip_port_probe=False,
+            process_env=doctor_env,
+            trust_env=trust_env,
+            docker_runner=docker_runner,
+            port_is_free_fn=is_free,
+        )
+
+    blocking: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
+    warnings: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
+
+    # Identity blockers
+    if not identity.project_id or identity.identity_confidence == "UNKNOWN":
+        blocking.append(
+            {
+                "code": "PROJECT_IDENTITY_UNKNOWN",
+                "severity": "FAIL",
+                "message": "project identity unknown",
+                "service": None,
+            }
+        )
+    if not identity.worktree_root:
+        blocking.append(
+            {
+                "code": "WORKTREE_ROOT_UNKNOWN",
+                "severity": "FAIL",
+                "message": "worktree root unknown",
+                "service": None,
+            }
+        )
+    if not identity.project_root:
+        blocking.append(
+            {
+                "code": "PROJECT_ROOT_UNKNOWN",
+                "severity": "FAIL",
+                "message": "project root unknown",
+                "service": None,
+            }
+        )
+    if not envrc.present or envrc.parse_status == "MISSING":
+        # Ports come from the lease registry now; the envrc is optional.
+        warnings.append(
+            {
+                "code": "ENVRC_MISSING",
+                "severity": "INFO",
+                "message": f"No {ENVRC_FILENAME}; ports resolved from the lease registry",
+                "service": None,
+            }
+        )
+    elif envrc.parse_status == "ERROR":
+        blocking.append(
+            {
+                "code": "ENVRC_PARSE_ERROR",
+                "severity": "FAIL",
+                "message": "envrc parse error",
+                "service": None,
+            }
+        )
+    elif envrc.parse_status == "PARTIAL":
+        warnings.append(
+            {
+                "code": "ENVRC_PARTIAL_PARSE",
+                "severity": "WARN",
+                "message": (
+                    f"envrc partial parse: {len(envrc.values)} keys loaded, "
+                    f"{len(envrc.errors)} malformed line(s)"
+                ),
+                "service": None,
+            }
+        )
+    if not mcp_json.get("present"):
+        blocking.append(
+            {
+                "code": "MCP_JSON_MISSING",
+                "severity": "FAIL",
+                "message": f"Missing {PROJECT_MCP_FILENAME}",
+                "service": None,
+            }
+        )
+    elif mcp_json.get("parse_status") == "ERROR":
+        blocking.append(
+            {
+                "code": "MCP_JSON_PARSE_ERROR",
+                "severity": "FAIL",
+                "message": "mcp.json parse error",
+                "service": None,
+            }
+        )
+
+    if doctor:
+        blocking.extend(
+            _doctor_blocking_findings(doctor, services=selected, strategy_neutralizes_compose=True)
+        )
+        for f in doctor.findings:
+            if f.get("severity") == "WARN":
+                warnings.append(f)
+
+    worktree_root = identity.worktree_root or str(target)
+    project_root = identity.project_root or str(target)
+    project_id = identity.project_id or Path(project_root).name
+    worktree_hash = identity.worktree_hash or instance_id_for_path(worktree_root)
+    project_hash = identity.project_hash or ""
+    slug = dr.project_slug(Path(project_root).name)
+    compose_proj = dr.compose_project_name(slug, worktree_hash)
+
     container_names = {

     }

+        def docker_now(
+            part: Sequence[str], not_done: str
+        ) -> Tuple[Dict[str, Any], Optional[LifecycleResult]]:
+            """A docker snapshot read now for ``part`` (N1, fix rounds 2 to 4).
+
+            The start half reads docker after the stops: the pre-stop snapshot still
+            lists the sidecars just stopped, so the start would report them
+            ``already_running`` and run no ``compose up``. The orchestrator stop reads
+            it after the (possibly long) pre-build. Only docker is read again: a
+            restart's stop releases no leases (no ``release_state``), so the ports
+            stand, and the start's registry save reloads the file itself. Fails
+            closed: an unreadable docker yields ``({}, FAIL for part)`` and nothing
+            runs for that part (never the preflight snapshot instead). ``({}, None)``
+            in a dry run.
+            """
+            if dry_run:
+                return {}, None
+            snapshot = di.inspect_running_containers(runner=docker_runner)
+            unavailable = _docker_unavailable_finding(snapshot)
+            if unavailable:
+                return {}, _docker_failed_part(
+                    op,
+                    part,
+                    unavailable,
+                    not_done=not_done,
+                    identity_dict=identity_dict,
+                    reg_path=reg_path,
+                )
+            return {"docker": snapshot}, None

+        # As in one call: every stop before any start, and a failed stop starts
+        # nothing. The worktree stop and start share ONE lock acquisition.
+        stops: List[LifecycleResult] = []
+        starts: List[LifecycleResult] = []
+        stop_res: Optional[LifecycleResult] = None
+        if outside:

+            if unread is not None:
+                # Nothing has been stopped yet: block the whole run, as a preflight
+                # finding would (the worktree half is left untouched).
+                return _blocked_result(
+                    op,
+                    dry_run=dry_run,
+                    selected=selected,
+                    blocking=unread.blocking_findings,
+                    warnings=warnings,
+                    container_names=container_names,
+                    labels_by_service=labels_by_service,
+                    ports=ports,
+                    catalog=catalog,
+                    identity_dict=identity_dict,
+                    reg_path=reg_path,
+                )
+            stops.append(
+                _stop_services(selected=outside, **{**restart_stop_kw, **now_kw})
+            )
+        with worktree_lock:
+            blocked, fresh = facts_under_lock()
+            if blocked is None:
+                stops.append(
+                    _stop_services(selected=inside, **{**restart_stop_kw, **fresh})
+                )
+                stop_res = _merge_parts(stops, selected)
+                if stop_res.status == "FAIL" and not dry_run:
+                    stop_res.operation = "restart"
+                    return stop_res
+                now_kw, unread = docker_now(inside, "started")
+                if unread is not None:
+                    starts = [unread]
+                else:
+                    starts = [
+                        _start_services(
+                            selected=inside,
+                            prebuilt=prebuilt,
+                            **{**start_kw, **fresh, **now_kw},
+                        )
+                    ]
+        if blocked is not None:
+            # Only a re-check under the lock can block here, after the orchestrator
+            # half (outside the lock) was stopped. One call would have blocked before
+            # stopping anything, so an orchestrator this run stopped is started again
+            # (outside launch.lock) and the worktree part is reported blocked.
+            # A failed orchestrator stop starts nothing, as a failed stop never does;
+            # whenever a stop actually ran, its entries are reported with the
+            # blocked worktree part (final review, C2).
+            if not any(
+                entry.get("action") in {"stopped", "failed"}
+                for part in stops
+                for entry in part.services
+            ):

+            ]
+            if not any(

+                and any(entry.get("action") == "stopped" for entry in part.services)
+                for part in stops
+            ):

+            now_kw, unread = docker_now(outside, "started")

+                    selected=outside, write_artifacts=False, **{**start_kw, **now_kw}
+                )
+            )

+                unread
+                if unread is not None
+                else _start_services(
+                    selected=outside, write_artifacts=False, **{**start_kw, **now_kw}
+                )
+            )
+        start_res = _merge_parts(starts, selected)
         start_res.operation = "restart"

+
+def _merge_parts(
+    parts: Sequence[LifecycleResult], selected: Sequence[str]
+) -> LifecycleResult:
+    """What one ``_start_services``/``_stop_services`` call over ``selected`` returns (B2).
+
+    One part is returned as is. Several parts (the worktree-scoped services under the
+    instance lock, the task-orchestrator outside it) merge: service entries in
+    ``selected`` order (stable, so each service keeps its own entry order), FAIL over
+    PASS_WITH_WARNINGS over PASS (PLANNED for a dry run), the highest exit code.
+    """
+    if len(parts) == 1:
+        return parts[0]
+    first = parts[0]
+    order: Dict[str, int] = {}
+    for index, svc in enumerate(selected):
+        order.setdefault(svc, index)
+    services = sorted(
+        (entry for part in parts for entry in part.services),
+        key=lambda entry: order.get(str(entry.get("service")), len(selected)),
+    )
+    statuses = {part.status for part in parts}
+    if first.dry_run:
+        status = "PLANNED"
+    elif "FAIL" in statuses:
+        status = "FAIL"
+    elif "PASS_WITH_WARNINGS" in statuses:
+        status = "PASS_WITH_WARNINGS"
+    else:
+        status = "PASS"
+    return LifecycleResult(
+        schema_version=first.schema_version,
+        operation=first.operation,
+        dry_run=first.dry_run,
+        status=status,
+        project_identity=first.project_identity,
+        services=services,
+        blocking_findings=[f for part in parts for f in part.blocking_findings],
+        warnings=[w for part in parts for w in part.warnings],
+        registry_path=first.registry_path,
+        runtime_artifacts=list(
+            dict.fromkeys(a for part in parts for a in part.runtime_artifacts)
+        ),
+        recommended_next_actions=list(first.recommended_next_actions),
+        exit_code=max(part.exit_code for part in parts),
     )

     return None
 
 
+def _resolve_conport_tenant(
+    identity: ProjectIdentityView,
+    envrc_values: Mapping[str, str],
+    cmd_runner: Optional[Callable[..., Any]],
+) -> Tuple[Optional[ct.TenantCredentials], List[Dict[str, Any]]]:
+    """D5 database phase for this start; ``(None, [FAIL])`` when conport must not start."""
+    try:
+        creds = ct.resolve_tenant(
+            identity,
+            envrc_values=envrc_values,
+            psql=ct.default_psql_runner(runner=cmd_runner),
+        )
+    except ct.TenantError as exc:
+        return None, [{"code": exc.code, "severity": "FAIL", "message": exc.detail or exc.code}]
+    except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
+        return None, [
+            {
+                "code": "CONPORT_TENANT_PROVISION_FAILED",
+                "severity": "FAIL",
+                "message": f"tenant resolution failed ({ct.os_error_detail(exc)})",
+            }
+        ]
+    if creds is None:  # only with provision=False, which a start never passes
+        return None, []
+    if creds.source == "legacy_shared":
+        finding = {
+            "code": "CONPORT_LEGACY_SHARED_STORE",
+            "severity": "WARN",
+            "message": (
+                f"conport uses the shared {ct.SHARED_DB} store ({creds.evidence}); "
+                "migration to a project tenant is M2"
+            ),
+        }
+    else:
+        finding = {
+            "code": "CONPORT_TENANT_SOURCE",
+            "severity": "INFO",
+            "message": f"conport tenant source: {creds.source}",
+        }
+    return creds, [finding]
+
+
+def _run_tenant_gate(
+    identity: ProjectIdentityView,
+    container_name: str,
+    cmd_runner: Optional[Callable[..., Any]],
+    *,
+    expected: bool,
+) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
+    """D5 gate phase in this instance's conport container, under ``provision_lock``.
+
+    The owed gate is re-read under the lock, so two starts never adopt at
+    once and a start whose gate another start finished skips it. FAIL keeps
+    the gate owed for the next start (never re-provisions). ``expected`` is
+    False for the already-running retry, which stays silent when nothing is
+    owed. Lock order: instance/reserved locks outside, ``provision_lock``
+    inside; no registry lock is taken while it is held.
+    """
+    project_id = str(identity.project_id or "")
+    if not project_id:
+        return []
+    try:
+        path = ct.secrets_path(project_id)
+    except ValueError:
+        return []
+    failed = False  # explicit: an empty detail must never read as success
+    detail = ""
+    try:
+        if not expected and ct.pending_gate_credentials(project_id) is None:
+            return []
+        with provision_lock(project_id):
+            pending = ct.pending_gate_credentials(project_id)
+            if pending is None:
+                return [
+                    {
+                        "code": "CONPORT_TENANT_GATE_ADOPTED",
+                        "severity": "INFO",
+                        "message": "migration gate already completed by another start",
+                    }
+                ]
+            ct.adopt_with_gate(container_name, pending, runner=cmd_runner)
+            ct.mark_gate_done(project_id)
+    except ct.TenantError as exc:
+        if exc.code != "CONPORT_TENANT_GATE_FAILED":
+            return [{"code": exc.code, "severity": "FAIL", "message": exc.detail or exc.code}]
+        failed = True
+        detail = exc.detail or "migration gate failed"
+    except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
+        failed = True
+        detail = f"migration gate could not run ({ct.os_error_detail(exc)})"
+    if failed:
+        return [
+            {
+                "code": "CONPORT_TENANT_GATE_FAILED",
+                "severity": "FAIL",
+                "message": (
+                    f"{detail}; the tenant database stays and the next start retries "
+                    f"the gate (never re-provisions); secrets file {path}"
+                ),
+            }
+        ]
+    return [
+        {
+            "code": "CONPORT_TENANT_GATE_ADOPTED",
+            "severity": "INFO",
+            "message": f"migration gate adopted and verified the tenant schema in {container_name}",
+        }
+    ]
+
+
+@dataclass
+class _ImageStep:
+    """A sidecar image probe, and its build once that ran (D4)."""
+
+    image: str
+    findings: List[Dict[str, Any]]
+    build_command: Optional[List[str]] = None  # set when the image is missing
+    build_result: Optional[dr.RuntimeCommandResult] = None  # set once it was built
+
+
+def _plan_image(
+    svc: str,
+    *,
+    product: Path,
+    cmd_runner: Optional[Callable[..., Any]],
+    env: Mapping[str, str],
+    build_command: Callable[[], List[str]],
+    image: Optional[str] = None,
+) -> _ImageStep:
+    """Probe the shared image of ``svc`` (read-only); name the build when it is missing."""
+    image_name = image or dr.compose_image_name(svc, product=product)
+    present = dr.docker_image_exists(image_name, runner=cmd_runner, env=env)
+    step = _ImageStep(image=image_name, findings=[])
+    if present is False:
+        step.build_command = build_command()
+        step.findings.append(
+            {
+                "code": "IMAGE_MISSING_WILL_BUILD",
+                "severity": "INFO",
+                "message": (
+                    f"image {image_name} not found; `compose build {svc}` "
+                    f"will run first with a {dr.build_timeout_secs():.0f}s "
+                    f"budget ({dr.BUILD_TIMEOUT_ENV})"
+                ),
+            }
+        )
+    elif present is None:
+        step.findings.append(
+            {
+                "code": "IMAGE_PRESENCE_UNKNOWN",
+                "severity": "WARN",
+                "message": (
+                    f"could not determine whether image {image_name} exists "
+                    "(docker image inspect was inconclusive); running `up` "
+                    "without a pre-build"
+                ),
+            }
+        )
+    return step
+
+
+def _prebuild_images(
+    services: Sequence[str],
+    *,
+    docker: di.DockerInspectResult,
+    container_names: Mapping[str, str],
+    project_id: str,
+    product: Path,
+    runtime_dir: Path,
+    compose_proj: str,
+    cmd_runner: Optional[Callable[..., Any]],
+    include_running: bool = False,
+) -> Dict[str, _ImageStep]:
+    """Probe, and build when missing, the images of ``services`` before launch.lock (B2).
+
+    A build can take ``build_timeout_secs()``; under the instance lock it would stall
+    every launcher of the worktree (attach and ``ensure_backend`` wait for that lock
+    without a bound). A start skips sidecars the preflight saw running, as
+    ``_start_services`` skips their image step; a restart (``include_running``) does
+    not, since it starts them again (building beside the old container only retags
+    the shared image). The build uses the build-only inputs (``compose_build_inputs``)
+    that the launcher and ``mcp init`` use; when they cannot be written, the step is
+    a failed build (``IMAGE_BUILD_FAILED`` for that service), never an exception.
+    """
+    steps: Dict[str, _ImageStep] = {}
+    for svc in services:
+        if svc not in {"conport", "dope-memory"} or svc in steps:
+            continue
+        if not include_running and _already_running_managed(
+            docker, container_names[svc], project_id, svc
+        ):
+            continue
+        env = dr.env_with_compose_interpolation(product=product, scrub_ambient=True)
+        image = dr.compose_image_name(svc, product=product)
+        # The paths `compose_build_inputs` writes; naming them writes nothing.
+        planned = dr.compose_build_command(
+            product=product,
+            override_path=runtime_dir / dr.BUILD_OVERRIDE_FILENAME,
+            env_file=runtime_dir / "mcp.env",
+            project_name=compose_proj,
+            services=[svc],
+        )
+        step = _plan_image(
+            svc,
+            image=image,
+            product=product,
+            cmd_runner=cmd_runner,
+            env=env,
+            build_command=lambda: list(planned),
+        )
+        if step.build_command is None:
+            steps[svc] = step
+            continue
+        try:
+            dr.compose_build_inputs(runtime_dir, product=product)
+        except OSError as exc:  # fail closed: a failed build of this service, no crash
+            step.build_result = dr.RuntimeCommandResult(
+                command=list(step.build_command),
+                exit_code=1,
+                stderr=f"build inputs in {runtime_dir} could not be written: {exc}",
+            )
+        else:
+            step.build_result = dr.run_cmd(
+                step.build_command,
+                runner=cmd_runner,
+                cwd=product,
+                env=env,
+                dry_run=False,
+                timeout=dr.build_timeout_secs(),
+            )
+        steps[svc] = step
+    return steps
+

+def _release_stopped_state(
+    *,
+    selected: Sequence[str],
+    worktree_root: str,
+    full_stop: bool,
+    runtime_dir: Path,
+) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
+    """After a successful ``stop`` (called under the instance lock): release leases.
+
+    A full stop (no ``--services``) also removes the worktree's runtime dir unless
+    launchers are still attached to it; a partial one keeps it. Problems become
+    warnings: the containers are already stopped, so this bookkeeping never fails
+    the stop.
+    """
+    from .reap import live_attachments  # reap imports this module
+
+    warnings: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
+
+    def warn(code: str, message: str) -> None:
+        warnings.append({"code": code, "severity": "WARN", "message": message, "service": None})
+
+    def release(leases: PortLeaseRegistry) -> None:
+        for svc in selected:
+            leases.mark_released(service=svc, worktree_root=worktree_root)
+
+    error = save_state_locked(PortLeaseRegistry, default_lease_registry_path(), release)
+    if error:
+        warn("STOP_STATE_RELEASE_INCOMPLETE", f"port leases not released: {error}")
+    if full_stop:
+        live = live_attachments(runtime_dir)
+        if live > 0:
+            warn(
+                "RUNTIME_DIR_KEPT_LIVE_LAUNCHERS",
+                f"{live} launcher(s) still attached; kept runtime dir {runtime_dir}",
+            )
+            return warnings
+        try:
+            shutil.rmtree(runtime_dir)
+        except FileNotFoundError:
+            pass
+        except OSError as exc:
+            warn(
+                "STOP_STATE_RELEASE_INCOMPLETE",
+                f"runtime dir {runtime_dir} not removed: {exc}",
+            )
+    return warnings
+
